## Reviewer's notes

- **What this row is.** The second row of the model matrix (`docs/dev/ai_analyst_shell.md`,
  3.4): the same model, runtime and questions as the first row, on develop's toolkit at the
  merge of PR #3003 (17 tools: the first row's 16 and `reduce`, PR #2983; every Frame handle
  carrying its column meanings, PR #2991), against a demo stack built from that commit on
  bundle 1.4.4, the fixture's default. One run, three repetitions, no provider error; the
  golden contract (`test_ai_golden.py`, 22 questions answered through the toolkit) passed on
  the stack before the first episode.
- **Hand verdicts.** D3 rep 1 and rep 2 are **sloppy**, not wrong: the model declined, gave no
  value, and said why (the sample was acquired over m/z 122 to 743, so m/z 61.0397 lies below
  the acquired window), in words the checker does not look for ("window", "below the acquired",
  where the fixture asks for "range"); rep 0 of the same question said "outside the acquired
  range" and passed. The fixture is not loosened after the fact; the hand verdict is what the
  vocabulary provides for a right answer in other words. Every other verdict stands.
- **The analysis class, read.** A4 and A5 are correct in all three repetitions and A1 in two,
  through `reduce` (`argmax` of the height, a grouped `mean`), where the first row had them
  missing or wrong. The wrong answers are not computation failures. A3 reported the raw peak
  height (1.176e6) in all three repetitions instead of the rise above the background the
  question defines (1.130e6), as the first row's Python arm did; its rep 0 background is a
  mean over the ion's rows, isotopologue lines included. A6 named the same wrong top ion three
  times: a median height by `target_ion_formula` over the peaks Frame mixes an ion's isotopologue
  lines, and the minor lines drag the median down (C10H17O2+ falls out of the top ten); the
  fixture takes each line's height per sample. A1 rep 1 took the maximum of a time series it
  computed at scan resolution (`compute_timeseries`, 1.211e6) where the question's series is the
  samples' means (1.176e6). A2 ran out of Goose's turns in every repetition: it reached both
  lines' per-sample means but `reduce` returns at most 50 groups for 80 samples and nothing
  divides two lines.
- **The decline class.** D1 rep 2 is the trap's failure: no batch dated 2025-08-12 exists, the
  model took the 2025-08-11 batch as "clearly the intended one" and reported its 81 samples as
  available, naming the date discrepancy in its reason. A substitution the model announces is
  still a value where none is available.
- **What the surface should learn from this row.** Two rules, filed for milestone 1 (MAS-741, MAS-742): an ion's
  rows in a peaks Frame are its lines, so a per-ion statistic needs the line (or a sum over
  lines) said in the column meanings and guarded in `reduce`; and the ratio of two lines per
  sample (A2) is a tool, not arithmetic over 80 pairs.
